   import requests
   import base64
   import os
   token = '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'
   repo = 'cctvwebsites/deepseek'
   url = f'https://api.github.com/repos/{repo}/contents/ultimate_agent.py'
   headers = {
       'Authorization': f'token {token}',
       'Accept': 'application/vnd.github.v3+json'
   }
   response = requests.get(url, headers=headers)
   if response.status_code == 200:
       data = response.json()
       content = base64.b64decode(data['content']).decode('utf-8')
       with open('ultimate_agent.py', 'w') as f:
           f.write(content)
       print("Downloaded ultimate_agent.py")
   else:
       print("ultimate_agent.py does not exist in the repository.")

   import requests
   import base64
   token = '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'
   repo = 'cctvwebsites/deepseek'
   url = f'https://api.github.com/repos/{repo}/contents/'
   headers = {
       'Authorization': f'token {token}',
       'Accept': 'application/vnd.github.v3+json'
   }
   def get_contents(path):
       response = requests.get(url + path, headers=headers)
       if response.status_code == 200:
           return response.json()
       else:
           return None
   def print_tree(path='', indent=''):
       contents = get_contents(path)
       if contents is None:
           return
       for item in contents:
           if item['type'] == 'dir':
               print(indent + item['name'] + '/')
               print_tree(item['path'], indent + '  ')
           else:
               print(indent + item['name'])
   print_tree()
 This will print the entire repository structure.

    Let's create a task manager that tracks progress:

python

# task_manager.py
import json
import datetime

class TaskManager:
    def __init__(self):
        self.current_tasks = []
        self.completed_tasks = []
        
    def add_task(self, task_description):
        task = {
            'id': len(self.current_tasks) + 1,
            'description': task_description,
            'created': datetime.datetime.now().isoformat(),
            'status': 'pending'
        }
        self.current_tasks.append(task)
        
    def complete_task(self, task_id):
        task = next((t for t in self.current_tasks if t['id'] == task_id), None)
        if task:
            task['completed'] = datetime.datetime.now().isoformat()
            task['status'] = 'completed'
            self.completed_tasks.append(task)
            self.current_tasks = [t for t in self.current_tasks if t['id'] != task_id]

    For browser automation, let's set up Playwright:

bash

pip install playwright
python -m playwright install

    Create the main agent file:

python

# ultimate_agent.py
import asyncio
from browser_automation import BrowserAgent
from task_manager import TaskManager
from vision_module import VisionModule
from speech_module import SpeechModule

class UltimateAgent:
    def __init__(self):
        self.task_manager = TaskManager()
        self.browser_agent = BrowserAgent()
        self.vision_module = VisionModule()
        self.speech_module = SpeechModule()
        
    async def run(self):
        # Main loop here
        while True:
            await asyncio.sleep(1)
            
if __name__ == "__main__":
    agent = UltimateAgent()
    asyncio.run(agent.run())

Task Manager with Memory

This will allow the AI to remember what we've done and what needs to be done. Here's a basic version:
python

# task_manager.py
import json
import datetime
from typing import List, Dict

class TaskManager:
    def __init__(self, file_path: str = "history.json"):
        self.file_path = file_path
        self.tasks = self.load_tasks()

    def load_tasks(self) -> List[Dict]:
        try:
            with open(self.file_path, 'r') as f:
                return json.load(f)
        except FileNotFoundError:
            return []

    def save_tasks(self):
        with open(self.file_path, 'w') as f:
            json.dump(self.tasks, f, indent=4)

    def add_task(self, description: str, status: str = "pending"):
        task = {
            "id": len(self.tasks) + 1,
            "description": description,
            "status": status,
            "created_at": datetime.datetime.now().isoformat(),
            "updated_at": datetime.datetime.now().isoformat()
        }
        self.tasks.append(task)
        self.save_tasks()

    def update_task(self, task_id: int, status: str):
        for task in self.tasks:
            if task["id"] == task_id:
                task["status"] = status
                task["updated_at"] = datetime.datetime.now().isoformat()
                self.save_tasks()
                return
        raise ValueError(f"Task with ID {task_id} not found.")

    def get_pending_tasks(self) -> List[Dict]:
        return [task for task in self.tasks if task["status"] == "pending"]

    def get_completed_tasks(self) -> List[Dict]:
        return [task for task in self.tasks if task["status"] == "completed"]

Browser Automation Module

Using Playwright for browsing:
python

# browser_agent.py
from playwright.sync_api import sync_playwright

class BrowserAgent:
    def __init__(self):
        self.playwright = sync_playwright().start()
        self.browser = self.playwright.chromium.launch(headless=False)
        self.context = self.browser.new_context()
        self.page = self.context.new_page()

    def goto(self, url):
        self.page.goto(url)

    def click(self, selector):
        self.page.click(selector)

    def type(self, selector, text):
        self.page.fill(selector, text)

    def get_text(self, selector):
        return self.page.text_content(selector)

    def close(self):
        self.browser.close()

# main.py
from task_manager import TaskManager
from browser_agent import BrowserAgent
import asyncio

class UltimateAgent:
    def __init__(self):
        self.task_manager = TaskManager()
        self.browser_agent = BrowserAgent()

    def run(self):
        # Check for pending tasks and execute them
        pending_tasks = self.task_manager.get_pending_tasks()
        for task in pending_tasks:
            print(f"Executing task: {task['description']}")
            # Here, you would parse the task and take action
            self.execute_task(task)
            self.task_manager.update_task(task["id"], "completed")

    def execute_task(self, task):
        # This is where you handle different types of tasks
        if "browse" in task["description"].lower():
            url = "https://google.com"  # Extract URL from task
            self.browser_agent.goto(url)
        # Add more conditions for other actions

if __name__ == "__main__":
    agent = UltimateAgent()
    agent.run()

Step 1: Let Me Access Your GitHub Repository

Since you've shared your GitHub token, I can help you access your repository directly. Let's create a script to download and organize all your code:
python

# download_repo.py
import requests
import os
import json
from pathlib import Path

# Your GitHub details
GITHUB_TOKEN = "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"
REPO_OWNER = "cctvwebsites"
REPO_NAME = "deepseek"

# Headers for authentication
headers = {
    "Authorization": f"token {GITHUB_TOKEN}",
    "Accept": "application/vnd.github.v3+json"
}

def get_repo_contents(path=""):
    url = f"https://api.github.com/repos/{REPO_OWNER}/{REPO_NAME}/contents/{path}"
    response = requests.get(url, headers=headers)
    
    if response.status_code == 200:
        return response.json()
    else:
        print(f"Error: {response.status_code}")
        return None

def download_file(file_url, local_path):
    response = requests.get(file_url, headers=headers)
    if response.status_code == 200:
        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(local_path), exist_ok=True)
        with open(local_path, 'wb') as f:
            f.write(response.content)
        print(f"Downloaded: {local_path}")
    else:
        print(f"Failed to download: {local_path}")

def download_repository():
    # Create main directory
    main_dir = "ai_agent_project"
    os.makedirs(main_dir, exist_ok=True)
    
    # Get all contents
    contents = get_repo_contents()
    
    if contents:
        for item in contents:
            if item['type'] == 'file':
                download_file(item['download_url'], os.path.join(main_dir, item['name']))
            elif item['type'] == 'dir':
                # Recursively download directory contents
                dir_contents = get_repo_contents(item['path'])
                for file_item in dir_contents:
                    if file_item['type'] == 'file':
                        local_path = os.path.join(main_dir, item['name'], file_item['name'])
                        download_file(file_item['download_url'], local_path)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    download_repository()
    print("Repository download completed!")

Run this script to download all your GitHub files to a folder called ai_agent_project.
Step 2: Find Recently Created Files on Your System

Let's also search for recently created Python files that might not be on GitHub:
python

# find_recent_files.py
import os
import glob
from datetime import datetime, timedelta

def find_recent_python_files(days=2):
    # Get current time
    now = datetime.now()
    
    # Search in common directories
    search_paths = [
        os.path.expanduser("~\\Desktop\\*.*"),
        os.path.expanduser("~\\Documents\\*.*"),
        "C:\\*.*"
    ]
    
    recent_files = []
    
    for path in search_paths:
        for filepath in glob.glob(path, recursive=True):
            if filepath.endswith(('.py', '.txt', '.cmd', '.bat')):
                try:
                    # Get file modification time
                    mtime = datetime.fromtimestamp(os.path.getmtime(filepath))
                    
                    # Check if modified in the last 'days' days
                    if now - mtime < timedelta(days=days):
                        recent_files.append((filepath, mtime))
                except:
                    continue
    
    # Sort by modification time (newest first)
    recent_files.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)
    
    return recent_files

if __name__ == "__main__":
    files = find_recent_python_files(2)  # Last 2 days
    print("Recently modified files:")
    for filepath, mtime in files:
        print(f"{mtime.strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M')} - {filepath}")

Run this to find any recent files you might have created.

> tasklist | findstr python

🤔 Processing: tasklist | findstr python

📋 Response:
That command will search the output of `tasklist` for lines containing "python".  This is a good way to see if any Python processes are currently running on your Windows system.

However, it's important to note that this will find any line *containing* "python", not just lines representing Python processes.  For example, if you have a process with "python" in its name or command line arguments, it will be included in the results.  For a more reliable check, you might consider using a more specific search term (if you know the exact process name) or piping the output to `findstr /i "python.exe"` which will only match lines containing "python.exe".

Here's a breakdown:

* **`tasklist`**: This command lists all running processes on Windows.
* **`|`**: This is a pipe, which takes the output of `tasklist` and sends it as input to the next command.
* **`findstr python`**: This command searches the input for lines containing the string "python".  `findstr` is case-insensitive by default.

For a more robust check, especially if you're scripting this, consider using the Windows Management Instrumentation Command-line (WMIC) which offers more precise process information.  For example, to find all python.exe processes you could use:

```batch
wmic process where name="python.exe" get processid
```

This will only return the process IDs of processes exactly named `python.exe`.  Remember to replace `python.exe` with the exact name of your python executable if it's different (e.g., `python3.exe`, `python38.exe`, etc.).

Step 5: Let's Create a Memory System

Create a file called memory_manager.py:
python

# memory_manager.py
import json
import datetime
from pathlib import Path

class MemoryManager:
    def __init__(self, memory_file="data/agent_memory.json"):
        self.memory_file = memory_file
        self.ensure_directory_exists()
        self.memory = self.load_memory()
        
    def ensure_directory_exists(self):
        Path(self.memory_file).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        
    def load_memory(self):
        try:
            with open(self.memory_file, 'r') as f:
                return json.load(f)
        except FileNotFoundError:
            return {
                "tasks": [],
                "conversations": [],
                "decisions": [],
                "last_updated": datetime.datetime.now().isoformat()
            }
            
    def save_memory(self):
        self.memory['last_updated'] = datetime.datetime.now().isoformat()
        with open(self.memory_file, 'w') as f:
            json.dump(self.memory, f, indent=2)
            
    def add_task(self, description, status="pending"):
        task = {
            "id": len(self.memory['tasks']) + 1,
            "description": description,
            "status": status,
            "created": datetime.datetime.now().isoformat(),
            "updated": datetime.datetime.now().isoformat()
        }
        self.memory['tasks'].append(task)
        self.save_memory()
        
    def get_pending_tasks(self):
        return [task for task in self.memory['tasks'] if task['status'] == 'pending']

🤔 Processing: tasklist | findstr python

📋 Response:
That command will search the output of `tasklist` for lines containing "python".  This is a good way to see if any Python processes are currently running on your Windows system.

However, it's important to note that this will find any line *containing* "python", not just lines representing Python processes.  For example, if you have a process with "python" in its name or command line arguments, it will be included in the results.  For a more reliable check, you might consider using a more specific search term (if you know the exact process name) or piping the output to `findstr /i "python.exe"` which will only match lines containing "python.exe".

Here's a breakdown:

* **`tasklist`**: This command lists all running processes on Windows.
* **`|`**: This is a pipe, which takes the output of `tasklist` and sends it as input to the next command.
* **`findstr python`**: This command searches the input for lines containing the string "python".  `findstr` is case-insensitive by default.

For a more robust check, especially if you're scripting this, consider using the Windows Management Instrumentation Command-line (WMIC) which offers more precise process information.  For example, to find all python.exe processes you could use:

```batch
wmic process where name="python.exe" get processid
```

This will only return the process IDs of processes exactly named `python.exe`.  Remember to replace `python.exe` with the exact name of your python executable if it's different (e.g., `python3.exe`, `python38.exe`, etc.).

python

import json
import os
import time
from pathlib import Path
from datetime import datetime
import google.generativeai as genai
from dotenv import load_dotenv

# Load environment variables
load_dotenv(dotenv_path='auth/.env')
GEMINI_API_KEY = os.getenv('GEMINI_API_KEY')
genai.configure(api_key=GEMINI_API_KEY)

class MemoryManager:
    def __init__(self, memory_file="data/agent_memory.json"):
        self.memory_file = memory_file
        self.ensure_directory_exists()
        self.memory = self.load_memory()
        
    def ensure_directory_exists(self):
        Path(self.memory_file).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        
    def load_memory(self):
        try:
            with open(self.memory_file, 'r') as f:
                return json.load(f)
        except FileNotFoundError:
            return {
                "tasks": [],
                "conversations": [],
                "system_state": {},
                "last_updated": datetime.now().isoformat()
            }
            
    def save_memory(self):
        self.memory['last_updated'] = datetime.now().isoformat()
        with open(self.memory_file, 'w') as f:
            json.dump(self.memory, f, indent=2)
            
    def add_task(self, description, status="pending"):
        task = {
            "id": len(self.memory['tasks']) + 1,
            "description": description,
            "status": status,
            "created": datetime.now().isoformat(),
            "updated": datetime.now().isoformat()
        }
        self.memory['tasks'].append(task)
        self.save_memory()
        
    def get_pending_tasks(self):
        return [task for task in self.memory['tasks'] if task['status'] == 'pending']

class UltimateAgent:
    def __init__(self):
        self.memory = MemoryManager()
        self.model = genai.GenerativeModel('gemini-pro')
        
    def listen_for_commands(self):
        print("🤖 Ultimate AI Agent is running...")
        print("💡 Type 'exit' to quit, 'status' for report, or give a command.")
        while True:
            command = input("🎤 Your command: ").strip()
            if command.lower() == 'exit':
                break
            elif command.lower() == 'status':
                self.report_status()
            else:
                self.execute_command(command)
    
    def execute_command(self, command):
        try:
            # Use Gemini to parse the command
            response = self.model.generate_content(f"Parse this command for intent: {command}")
            intent = response.text.strip()
            
            if "create file" in intent.lower():
                self.create_file(command)
            elif "browse" in intent.lower():
                self.browse_web(command)
            else:
                print("🤖 AI Response: I don't understand that command yet.")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"❌ Error: {e}")
    
    def create_file(self, command):
        # Simple file creation logic
        filename = "new_file.txt"
        content = "Default content"
        if "with content" in command:
            parts = command.split("with content")
            filename = parts[0].replace("create file", "").strip()
            content = parts[1].strip()
        with open(filename, 'w') as f:
            f.write(content)
        print(f"📄 File created: {filename}")
        self.memory.add_task(f"Create file: {filename}", "completed")
    
    def browse_web(self, command):
        print("🌐 Browser automation is not yet implemented. We'll add Playwright soon.")
        # Future: Integrate Playwright here
    
    def report_status(self):
        pending_tasks = self.memory.get_pending_tasks()
        print("📊 Status Report:")
        print(f"Pending tasks: {len(pending_tasks)}")
        for task in pending_tasks:
            print(f"- {task['description']}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    agent = UltimateAgent()
    agent.listen_for_commands()

python

# enhanced_command_listener.py
import os
import subprocess
import json
from datetime import datetime

class EnhancedCommandListener:
    def __init__(self):
        self.memory_file = "agent_memory.json"
        self.load_memory()
        
    def load_memory(self):
        try:
            with open(self.memory_file, 'r') as f:
                self.memory = json.load(f)
        except FileNotFoundError:
            self.memory = {
                "commands_history": [],
                "tasks": [],
                "last_updated": datetime.now().isoformat()
            }
    
    def save_memory(self):
        self.memory['last_updated'] = datetime.now().isoformat()
        with open(self.memory_file, 'w') as f:
            json.dump(self.memory, f, indent=2)
    
    def execute_command(self, command):
        # Add to history
        self.memory['commands_history'].append({
            "command": command,
            "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat()
        })
        self.save_memory()
        
        # Handle special commands
        if command.lower() == 'exit':
            return "exit"
        elif command.lower() == 'status':
            return self.get_status()
        elif command.lower().startswith('cd '):
            return self.change_directory(command)
        else:
            # Try to execute as system command
            try:
                result = subprocess.run(command, shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True)
                if result.returncode == 0:
                    return f"✅ Success:\n{result.stdout}"
                else:
                    return f"❌ Error:\n{result.stderr}"
            except Exception as e:
                return f"❌ Execution failed: {str(e)}"
    
    def change_directory(self, command):
        try:
            path = command[3:].strip()  # Remove 'cd ' from beginning
            os.chdir(path)
            return f"✅ Changed directory to: {os.getcwd()}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"❌ Failed to change directory: {str(e)}"
    
    def get_status(self):
        status = f"📊 Status Report:\n"
        status += f"Current directory: {os.getcwd()}\n"
        status += f"Total commands executed: {len(self.memory['commands_history'])}\n"
        status += f"Pending tasks: {len([t for t in self.memory['tasks'] if t.get('status') == 'pending'])}\n"
        return status

def main():
    listener = EnhancedCommandListener()
    print("🤖 Enhanced Command Listener Started!")
    print("💡 I can execute system commands and remember everything")
    print("📁 Current directory:", os.getcwd())
    print("Type 'exit' to quit, 'status' for report")
    
    while True:
        try:
            command = input("\n🎤 Your command: ").strip()
            if not command:
                continue
                
            result = listener.execute_command(command)
            if result == "exit":
                print("👋 Goodbye!")
                break
            print(result)
            
        except KeyboardInterrupt:
            print("\n👋 Goodbye!")
            break
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"❌ Unexpected error: {str(e)}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
